App-wide constants\n│   ├── types/                  # TypeScript type definitions\n│   │   ├── user.ts\n│   │   ├── project.ts\n│   │   ├── application.ts\n│   │   ├── milestone.ts\n│   │   └── api.ts              # API response types\n│   ├── styles/                 # Global styles\n│   │   ├── globals.css         # Global stylesheet\n│   │   └── variables.css       # CSS custom properties (design tokens)\n│   └── context/                # React Context providers\n│       ├── AuthContext.tsx\n│       └── ThemeContext.tsx\n├── next.config.js\n├── tsconfig.json\n├── package.json\n├── .env.local\n└── .env.example\n\`\`\`\n\n### 2.3 Backend Structure\n\n\`\`\`\nbackend/\n├── src/\n│   ├── config/                 # Configuration\n│   │   ├── database.ts         # DB connection\n│   │   ├── env.ts              # Environment validation (Zod)\n│   │   └── cors.ts             # CORS setup\n│   ├── modules/                # Feature modules\n│   │   ├── auth/\n│   │   │   ├── auth.controller.ts\n│   │   │   ├── auth.service.ts\n│   │   │   ├── auth.routes.ts\n│   │   │   ├── auth.validation.ts\n│   │   │   └── auth.types.ts\n│   │   ├── user/\n│   │   │   ├── user.controller.ts\n│   │   │   ├── user.service.ts\n│   │   │   ├── user.routes.ts\n│   │   │   ├── user.validation.ts\n│   │   │   └── user.types.ts\n│   │   ├── project/\n│   │   │   ├── project.controller.ts\n│   │   │   ├── project.service.ts\n│   │   │   ├── project.routes.ts\n│   │   │   ├── project.validation.ts\n│   │   │   └── project.types.ts\n│   │   ├── application/\n│   │   │   ├── application.controller.ts\n│   │   │   ├── application.service.ts\n│   │   │   ├── application.routes.ts\n│   │   │   ├── application.validation.ts\n│   │   │   └── application.types.ts\n│   │   ├── milestone/\n│   │   │   ├── milestone.controller.ts\n│   │   │   ├── milestone.service.ts\n│   │   │   ├── milestone.routes.ts\n│   │   │   ├── milestone.validation.ts\n│   │   │   └── milestone.types.ts\n│   │   ├── matching/\n│   │   │   ├── matching.controller.ts\n│   │   │   ├── matching.service.ts\n│   │   │   ├── matching.routes.ts\n│   │   │   └── matching.types.ts\n│   │   ├── escrow/\n│   │   │   ├── escrow.controller.ts\n│   │   │   ├── escrow.service.ts\n│   │   │   ├── escrow.routes.ts\n│   │   │   └── escrow.types.ts\n│   │   ├── portfolio/\n│   │   │   ├── portfolio.controller.ts\n│   │   │   ├── portfolio.service.ts\n│   │   │   ├── portfolio.routes.ts\n│   │   │   └── portfolio.types.ts\n│   │   └── certificate/\n│   │       ├── certificate.controller.ts\n│   │       ├── certificate.service.ts\n│   │       ├── certificate.routes.ts\n│   │       └── certificate.types.ts\n│   ├── middleware/             # Shared middleware\n│   │   ├── auth.middleware.ts\n│   │   ├── role.middleware.ts\n│   │   ├── validation.middleware.ts\n│   │   ├── rateLimiter.middleware.ts\n│   │   └── error.middleware.ts\n│   ├── utils/                  # Shared utilities\n│   │   ├── logger.ts\n│   │   ├── response.ts\n│   │   ├── errors.ts\n│   │   └── pagination.ts\n│   ├── prisma/                 # Prisma ORM\n│   │   ├── schema.prisma\n│   │   ├── seed.ts\n│   │   └── migrations/\n│   ├── app.ts                  # Express app setup\n│   └── server.ts               # Entry point\n├── tests/\n│   ├── unit/\n│   │   ├── services/\n│   │   └── utils/\n│   └── integration/\n│       └── routes/\n├── tsconfig.json\n├── package.json\n├── jest.config.ts\n├── .env\n└── .env.example\n\`\`\`\n\n### 2.4 File Organization Rules\n\n| Rule                                       | Rationale                                              |\n| :----------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------- |\n| One component per file                     | Easy to locate and maintain                            |\n| File name matches the default export name  | `ProjectCard.tsx` exports `ProjectCard`                |\n| Group by feature/domain, not by file type  | `modules/project/` instead of `controllers/project.ts` |\n| Use barrel exports (`index.ts`)            | Clean imports: `from '@/components/common'`            |\n| Keep files under 300 lines                 | If longer, refactor into smaller units                 |\n| Co-locate tests with source files (optional) | Or use a dedicated `tests/` directory             |\n\n---\n\n## 3. Naming Conventions\n\n### 3.1 General Rules\n\n| Element                | Convention         | Example                                    |\n| :--------------------- | :----------------- | :----------------------------------------- |\n| **Files (Component)**  | PascalCase         | `ProjectCard.tsx`, `MilestoneList.tsx`      |\n| **Files (Non-component)** | camelCase       | `useProjects.ts`, `auth.service.ts`        |\n| **Files (Config/Style)** | kebab-case       | `next.config.js`, `globals.css`            |\n| **Directories**        | kebab-case / camelCase | `common/`, `milestone/`, `utils/`       |\n| **Variables**          | camelCase          | `projectList`, `isLoading`, `currentUser`  |\n| **Constants**          | UPPER_SNAKE_CASE   | `MAX_TEAM_SIZE`, `JWT_EXPIRES_IN`          |\n| **Functions**          | camelCase          | `getProjectById()`, `validateInput()`      |\n| **Classes**            | PascalCase         | `AppError`, `ProjectService`               |\n| **Interfaces/Types**   | PascalCase         | `IProject`, `UserRole`, `ApiResponse<T>`   |\n| **Enums**              | PascalCase         | `ProjectStatus`, `EscrowStatus`            |\n| **Enum Members**       | UPPER_SNAKE_CASE   | `ProjectStatus.IN_PROGRESS`                |\n| **React Components**   | PascalCase         | `<ProjectCard />`, `<MilestoneList />`     |\n| **Custom Hooks**       | camelCase, `use` prefix | `useAuth()`, `useProjects()`          |\n| **CSS Classes**        | camelCase (modules) | `.projectCard`, `.milestoneTitle`          |\n| **Database Tables**    | snake_case (plural) | `users`, `projects`, `student_profiles`   |\n| **Database Columns**   | snake_case          | `created_at`, `user_id`, `full_name`      |\n| **API Endpoints**      | kebab-case (plural nouns) | `/api/v1/projects`, `/api/v1/student-groups` |\n| **Environment Variables** | UPPER_SNAKE_CASE | `DATABASE_URL`, `JWT_SECRET`              |\n\n### 3.2 Naming Best Practices\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD — Descriptive and consistent\nconst projectList: Project[] = [];\nconst isProjectLoading: boolean = false;\nconst handleProjectSubmit = () => {};\nconst MAX_MILESTONES_PER_PROJECT = 10;\n\n// ❌ BAD — Vague, inconsistent\nconst data: any[] = [];\nconst flag: boolean = false;\nconst doStuff = () => {};\nconst maxMilestones = 10;\n\`\`\`\n\n### 3.3 Boolean Naming\n\nAlways use `is`, `has`, `can`, `should`, or `will` prefixes for booleans:\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD\nconst isAuthenticated = true;\nconst hasApplied = false;\nconst canEdit = user.role === 'SME';\nconst shouldShowModal = errors.length > 0;\n\n// ❌ BAD\nconst authenticated = true;\nconst applied = false;\nconst edit = user.role === 'SME';\n\`\`\`\n\n### 3.4 Function Naming\n\nUse verb-first naming for functions:\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD — verb + noun\nfunction getProjectById(id: string): Promise<Project> {}\nfunction createApplication(data: CreateApplicationDTO): Promise<Application> {}\nfunction validateMilestoneData(data: unknown): MilestoneInput {}\nfunction isProjectOwner(userId: string, projectId: string): boolean {}\n\n// ❌ BAD — noun-first or vague\nfunction projectGet(id: string) {}\nfunction applicationCreate(data: any) {}\nfunction check(data: unknown) {}\n\`\`\`\n\n---\n\n## 4. TypeScript & JavaScript Standards\n\n### 4.1 TypeScript Configuration\n\n\`\`\`jsonc\n// tsconfig.json — strict mode enabled\n{\n  \"compilerOptions\": {\n    \"strict\": true,                   // Enable all strict type-checking\n    \"noImplicitAny\": true,            // No implicit 'any' types\n    \"strictNullChecks\": true,         // Null/undefined must be handled\n    \"noUnusedLocals\": true,           // Flag unused variables\n    \"noUnusedParameters\": true,       // Flag unused parameters\n    \"noImplicitReturns\": true,        // All code paths must return\n    \"esModuleInterop\": true,\n    \"resolveJsonModule\": true,\n    \"moduleResolution\": \"node\",\n    \"target\": \"ES2022\",\n    \"module\": \"ESNext\"\n  }\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 4.2 Type Safety Rules\n\n| Rule                                      | Enforcement |\n| :---------------------------------------- | :---------: |\n| **Never use `any`**                       | ❌ Forbidden |\n| Use `unknown` for truly unknown types     | ✅ Preferred |\n| Define interfaces for all API responses   | ✅ Required  |\n| Define DTOs for all request bodies        | ✅ Required  |\n| Use `enum` or union types for fixed sets  | ✅ Required  |\n| Avoid type assertions (`as`)              | ⚠️ Minimize |\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD — Explicit types, no 'any'\ninterface CreateProjectDTO {\n  title: string;\n  description: string;\n  requiredSkills: string[];\n  budget: number;\n  durationWeeks: number;\n  milestones: CreateMilestoneDTO[];\n}\n\nasync function createProject(data: CreateProjectDTO): Promise<Project> {\n  // ...implementation\n}\n\n// ❌ BAD — Using 'any'\nasync function createProject(data: any): Promise<any> {\n  // ...\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 4.3 Code Style Rules\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// 1. Use const by default, let only when reassignment is needed\nconst projects = await getProjects();    // ✅\nlet currentPage = 1;                     // ✅ (will be reassigned)\nvar oldStyle = 'bad';                    // ❌ Never use var\n\n// 2. Use arrow functions for callbacks\nconst activeProjects = projects.filter((p) => p.status === 'PUBLISHED');  // ✅\nconst activeProjects = projects.filter(function(p) { return p.status === 'PUBLISHED'; });  // ❌\n\n// 3. Use template literals instead of string concatenation\nconst message = `Project ${project.title} has been accepted`;  // ✅\nconst message = 'Project ' + project.title + ' has been accepted';  // ❌\n\n// 4. Use destructuring\nconst { title, description, budget } = project;  // ✅\nconst title = project.title;  // ❌ (when accessing multiple properties)\n\n// 5. Use optional chaining and nullish coalescing\nconst companyName = smeProfile?.companyName ?? 'Unknown';  // ✅\nconst companyName = smeProfile && smeProfile.companyName ? smeProfile.companyName : 'Unknown';  // ❌\n\n// 6. Use async/await instead of .then() chains\nconst project = await projectService.getById(id);  // ✅\nprojectService.getById(id).then(project => {});     // ❌\n\n// 7. Prefer early returns to reduce nesting\nasync function getProject(id: string): Promise<Project> {\n  const project = await prisma.project.findUnique({ where: { id } });\n  if (!project) {\n    throw new NotFoundError('Project');  // ✅ Early return\n  }\n  return project;\n}\n\n// ❌ BAD — Deep nesting\nasync function getProject(id: string): Promise<Project> {\n  const project = await prisma.project.findUnique({ where: { id } });\n  if (project) {\n    return project;\n  } else {\n    throw new NotFoundError('Project');\n  }\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 4.4 Import Ordering\n\nImports MUST be ordered in the following groups, separated by blank lines:\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// 1. Node built-in modules\nimport path from 'path';\nimport fs from 'fs';\n\n// 2. Third-party packages\nimport express from 'express';\nimport { z } from 'zod';\nimport jwt from 'jsonwebtoken';\n\n// 3. Internal modules (absolute imports)\nimport { ProjectService } from '@/modules/project/project.service';\nimport { AppError } from '@/utils/errors';\n\n// 4. Relative imports\nimport { CreateProjectDTO } from './project.types';\nimport { projectValidation } from './project.validation';\n\`\`\`\n\n---\n\n## 5. React & Next.js Conventions\n\n### 5.1 Component Structure\n\nEvery React component SHOULD follow this structure:\n\n\`\`\`tsx\n// 1. Imports\nimport { useState, useEffect } from 'react';\nimport { ProjectCard } from '@/components/project';\nimport { useProjects } from '@/hooks/useProjects';\nimport type { Project } from '@/types/project';\nimport styles from './ProjectList.module.css';\n\n// 2. Type definitions (if local)\ninterface ProjectListProps {\n  category?: string;\n  maxItems?: number;\n}\n\n// 3. Component definition (named export preferred)\nexport function ProjectList({ category, maxItems = 10 }: ProjectListProps) {\n  // 3a. Hooks (top of component, in consistent order)\n  const { projects, isLoading, error } = useProjects({ category, limit: maxItems });\n  const [selectedId, setSelectedId] = useState<string | null>(null);\n\n  // 3b. Derived state / memoized values\n  const activeProjects = projects.filter((p) => p.status === 'PUBLISHED');\n\n  // 3c. Event handlers\n  const handleProjectClick = (id: string) => {\n    setSelectedId(id);\n  };\n\n  // 3d. Effects\n  useEffect(() => {\n    // Side effects\n  }, [category]);\n\n  // 3e. Early returns (loading, error states)\n  if (isLoading) return <Spinner />;\n  if (error) return <ErrorMessage message={error.message} />;\n\n  // 3f. Render\n  return (\n    <div className={styles.container}>\n      {activeProjects.map((project) => (\n        <ProjectCard\n          key={project.id}\n          project={project}\n          onClick={() => handleProjectClick(project.id)}\n        />\n      ))}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 5.2 Component Rules\n\n| Rule                                              | Rationale                                     |\n| :------------------------------------------------ | :-------------------------------------------- |\n| One component per file                            | Clarity and maintainability                   |\n| Use functional components (no class components)   | Modern React standard                         |\n| Define props with TypeScript interfaces            | Type safety                                   |\n| Use named exports (not default exports)           | Better refactoring and import clarity          |\n| Keep components under 150 lines                   | If longer, extract sub-components              |\n| Extract complex logic into custom hooks           | Separation of concerns                        |\n| Use CSS Modules for styling                       | Scoped styles, no conflicts                   |\n\n### 5.3 Hooks Rules\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD — Custom hook for data fetching\nexport function useProjects(filters?: ProjectFilters) {\n  const [projects, setProjects] = useState<Project[]>([]);\n  const [isLoading, setIsLoading] = useState(true);\n  const [error, setError] = useState<Error | null>(null);\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    const fetchProjects = async () => {\n      try {\n        setIsLoading(true);\n        const response = await api.get('/projects', { params: filters });\n        setProjects(response.data.data);\n      } catch (err) {\n        setError(err instanceof Error ? err : new Error('Unknown error'));\n      } finally {\n        setIsLoading(false);\n      }\n    };\n\n    fetchProjects();\n  }, [filters]);\n\n  return { projects, isLoading, error };\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 5.4 Page Component Conventions (Next.js App Router)\n\n\`\`\`tsx\n// app/projects/page.tsx\nimport { ProjectList } from '@/components/project';\n\n// Page-level metadata (Next.js)\nexport const metadata = {\n  title: 'Browse Projects — SkillBridge',\n  description: 'Find real projects from SMEs and build your portfolio',\n};\n\n// Page component (server component by default)\nexport default function ProjectsPage() {\n  return (\n    <main>\n      <h1>Browse Projects</h1>\n      <ProjectList />\n    </main>\n  );\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n---\n\n## 6. Backend (Node.js / Express) Conventions\n\n### 6.1 Module Structure\n\nEach backend feature module follows a strict pattern:\n\n| File                    | Responsibility                                              |\n| :---------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `*.routes.ts`           | Define Express routes, attach middleware and controller       |\n| `*.controller.ts`       | Parse request, call service, send response                   |\n| `*.service.ts`          | Business logic, database access via Prisma                   |\n| `*.validation.ts`       | Zod schemas for request validation                           |\n| `*.types.ts`            | TypeScript types/interfaces for the module                   |\n\n### 6.2 Controller Pattern\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// project.controller.ts\nimport { Request, Response, NextFunction } from 'express';\nimport { ProjectService } from './project.service';\nimport { sendSuccess, sendCreated } from '@/utils/response';\n\nconst projectService = new ProjectService();\n\nexport class ProjectController {\n  /**\n   * GET /api/v1/projects\n   * List published projects with optional filters.\n   */\n  async getProjects(req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) {\n    try {\n      const { page, limit, skills, status } = req.query;\n      const result = await projectService.getProjects({\n        page: Number(page) || 1,\n        limit: Number(limit) || 10,\n        skills: skills ? String(skills).split(',') : undefined,\n        status: status as string | undefined,\n      });\n      sendSuccess(res, result.data, result.meta);\n    } catch (error) {\n      next(error);\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * POST /api/v1/projects\n   * Create a new project (SME only).\n   */\n  async createProject(req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) {\n    try {\n      const smeId = req.user!.id;  // Attached by auth middleware\n      const project = await projectService.createProject(smeId, req.body);\n      sendCreated(res, project);\n    } catch (error) {\n      next(error);\n    }\n  }\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n> [!IMPORTANT]\n> **Controller rules**: \n> - Controllers MUST NOT contain business logic.\n> - Controllers MUST NOT access the database directly.\n> - Controllers MUST delegate all logic to the service layer.\n> - Controllers MUST use `next(error)` for error propagation.\n\n### 6.3 Service Pattern\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// project.service.ts\nimport { PrismaClient, ProjectStatus } from '@prisma/client';\nimport { CreateProjectDTO, ProjectFilters } from './project.types';\nimport { NotFoundError, ForbiddenError } from '@/utils/errors';\n\nconst prisma = new PrismaClient();\n\nexport class ProjectService {\n  /**\n   * Get paginated list of projects with optional filters.\n   */\n  async getProjects(filters: ProjectFilters) {\n    const { page, limit, skills, status } = filters;\n    const skip = (page - 1) * limit;\n\n    const where = {\n      ...(status && { status: status as ProjectStatus }),\n      ...(skills && {\n        requiredSkills: { hasSome: skills },\n      }),\n    };\n\n    const [data, total] = await Promise.all([\n      prisma.project.findMany({\n        where,\n        skip,\n        take: limit,\n        orderBy: { createdAt: 'desc' },\n        include: { sme: { select: { companyName: true } } },\n      }),\n      prisma.project.count({ where }),\n    ]);\n\n    return {\n      data,\n      meta: {\n        total,\n        page,\n        limit,\n        totalPages: Math.ceil(total / limit),\n      },\n    };\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Create a new project.\n   */\n  async createProject(smeId: string, dto: CreateProjectDTO) {\n    return prisma.project.create({\n      data: {\n        smeId,\n        title: dto.title,\n        description: dto.description,\n        requiredSkills: dto.requiredSkills,\n        budget: dto.budget,\n        durationWeeks: dto.durationWeeks,\n        status: 'PENDING_REVIEW',\n        escrowStatus: 'NONE',\n        milestones: {\n          create: dto.milestones.map((m, index) => ({\n            title: m.title,\n            description: m.description,\n            deadline: m.deadline,\n            orderIndex: index,\n            status: 'NOT_STARTED',\n          })),\n        },\n      },\n      include: { milestones: true },\n    });\n  }\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n> [!IMPORTANT]\n> **Service rules**:\n> - Services MUST NOT access `req` or `res` objects.\n> - Services contain ALL business logic and validation rules.\n> - Services interact with the database exclusively through Prisma.\n> - Services throw custom `AppError` subclasses for error conditions.\n\n### 6.4 Route Definition Pattern\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// project.routes.ts\nimport { Router } from 'express';\nimport { ProjectController } from './project.controller';\nimport { authMiddleware } from '@/middleware/auth.middleware';\nimport { roleMiddleware } from '@/middleware/role.middleware';\nimport { validate } from '@/middleware/validation.middleware';\nimport { createProjectSchema, projectQuerySchema } from './project.validation';\n\nconst router = Router();\nconst controller = new ProjectController();\n\n// Public routes (authenticated users)\nrouter.get(\n  '/',\n  authMiddleware,\n  validate(projectQuerySchema, 'query'),\n  controller.getProjects.bind(controller)\n);\n\nrouter.get(\n  '/:id',\n  authMiddleware,\n  controller.getProjectById.bind(controller)\n);\n\n// SME-only routes\nrouter.post(\n  '/',\n  authMiddleware,\n  roleMiddleware('SME'),\n  validate(createProjectSchema),\n  controller.createProject.bind(controller)\n);\n\nexport { router as projectRoutes };\n\`\`\`\n\n### 6.5 Validation Pattern (Zod)\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// project.validation.ts\nimport { z } from 'zod';\n\nexport const createProjectSchema = z.object({\n  title: z.string().min(5).max(200),\n  description: z.string().min(20).max(5000),\n  requiredSkills: z.array(z.string()).min(1).max(10),\n  budget: z.number().positive().max(50_000_000),\n  durationWeeks: z.number().int().min(1).max(8),\n  milestones: z.array(z.object({\n    title: z.string().min(3).max(200),\n    description: z.string().min(10).max(2000),\n    deadline: z.string().datetime(),\n  })).min(1).max(10),\n});\n\nexport const projectQuerySchema = z.object({\n  page: z.string().optional().transform(Number).pipe(z.number().int().positive()).optional(),\n  limit: z.string().optional().transform(Number).pipe(z.number().int().min(1).max(50)).optional(),\n  skills: z.string().optional(),\n  status: z.enum(['PUBLISHED', 'MATCHED', 'IN_PROGRESS', 'COMPLETED']).optional(),\n});\n\`\`\`\n\n---\n\n## 7. SOLID Principles — Applied to SkillBridge\n\n### 7.1 Overview\n\nThe **SOLID** principles guide the design of the SkillBridge codebase to ensure modularity, testability, and long-term maintainability.\n\n### 7.2 S — Single Responsibility Principle (SRP)\n\n> **\"A module should have one, and only one, reason to change.\"**\n\nEach file/class/function has a **single, well-defined responsibility**:\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD — Each layer has one responsibility\n\n// project.controller.ts — ONLY handles HTTP request/response\nexport class ProjectController {\n  async getProjects(req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) {\n    try {\n      const result = await this.projectService.getProjects(req.query);\n      sendSuccess(res, result);\n    } catch (error) {\n      next(error);\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// project.service.ts — ONLY handles business logic\nexport class ProjectService {\n  async getProjects(filters: ProjectFilters) {\n    // Business logic + data access\n  }\n}\n\n// project.validation.ts — ONLY handles input validation\nexport const createProjectSchema = z.object({ /* ... */ });\n\`\`\`\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ❌ BAD — Controller doing everything (validation + business logic + DB access)\nrouter.post('/projects', async (req, res) => {\n  if (!req.body.title) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Title required' });\n  const project = await prisma.project.create({ data: req.body });\n  // Send email\n  await sendEmail(project.smeId, 'Project created');\n  res.json(project);\n});\n\`\`\`\n\n**Application to SkillBridge modules:**\n\n| Module        | Controller         | Service                  | Validation                |\n| :------------ | :----------------- | :----------------------- | :------------------------ |\n| `auth`        | Parse login/register requests | Hash passwords, issue JWT | Validate credentials     |\n| `project`     | Handle project CRUD routes | Project business rules   | Validate project data     |\n| `application` | Handle apply/withdraw routes | Apply logic, duplicate check | Validate application  |\n| `milestone`   | Handle milestone updates | Status transitions       | Validate milestone data   |\n| `escrow`      | Handle escrow status routes | Escrow state machine     | —                         |\n| `portfolio`   | Handle portfolio queries | Portfolio generation     | —                         |\n| `certificate` | Handle cert generation | PDF creation logic       | —                         |\n\n### 7.3 O — Open/Closed Principle (OCP)\n\n> **\"Software entities should be open for extension, but closed for modification.\"**\n\nDesign modules so that new behavior can be **added without modifying existing code**.\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD — Matching strategy is extensible without modifying MatchingService\n\n// matching/strategies/base.strategy.ts\nexport interface MatchingStrategy {\n  findMatches(project: Project, candidates: Student[]): ScoredStudent[];\n}\n\n// matching/strategies/skillTag.strategy.ts  (MVP)\nexport class SkillTagMatchingStrategy implements MatchingStrategy {\n  findMatches(project: Project, candidates: Student[]): ScoredStudent[] {\n    // Simple skill-tag overlap scoring\n    return candidates.map(student => ({\n      student,\n      score: this.calculateSkillOverlap(project.requiredSkills, student.skills),\n    })).sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score);\n  }\n}\n\n// matching/strategies/ruleBased.strategy.ts  (V1.1 — added without modifying existing)\nexport class RuleBasedMatchingStrategy implements MatchingStrategy {\n  findMatches(project: Project, candidates: Student[]): ScoredStudent[] {\n    // Advanced rule-based scoring (skills + history + rating)\n  }\n}\n\n// matching/strategies/ai.strategy.ts  (V2.0 — added without modifying existing)\nexport class AIMatchingStrategy implements MatchingStrategy {\n  findMatches(project: Project, candidates: Student[]): ScoredStudent[] {\n    // ML-based matching\n  }\n}\n\n// matching/matching.service.ts — uses strategy via dependency injection\nexport class MatchingService {\n  constructor(private strategy: MatchingStrategy) {}\n\n  async getRecommendations(projectId: string): Promise<ScoredStudent[]> {\n    const project = await this.getProject(projectId);\n    const candidates = await this.getCandidates(project);\n    return this.strategy.findMatches(project, candidates);\n  }\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 7.4 L — Liskov Substitution Principle (LSP)\n\n> **\"Subtypes must be substitutable for their base types.\"**\n\nAny implementation of an interface can be used interchangeably without breaking the system:\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD — Any MatchingStrategy can replace another\n\n// In MVP\nconst matchingService = new MatchingService(new SkillTagMatchingStrategy());\n\n// In V1.1 — just swap the strategy, service code unchanged\nconst matchingService = new MatchingService(new RuleBasedMatchingStrategy());\n\n// In V2.0 — same pattern\nconst matchingService = new MatchingService(new AIMatchingStrategy());\n\`\`\`\n\n**Application**: The escrow module follows the same pattern:\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// escrow/escrow.provider.ts\nexport interface EscrowProvider {\n  deposit(projectId: string, amount: number): Promise<EscrowStatus>;\n  disburse(projectId: string): Promise<EscrowStatus>;\n  getStatus(projectId: string): Promise<EscrowStatus>;\n}\n\n// MVP: Simulated\nexport class SimulatedEscrowProvider implements EscrowProvider {\n  async deposit(projectId: string, amount: number) {\n    // Just update status flag in database\n    return 'DEPOSITED';\n  }\n}\n\n// V1.1: Real (VNPay/Momo) — swap without modifying service\nexport class VNPayEscrowProvider implements EscrowProvider {\n  async deposit(projectId: string, amount: number) {\n    // Real payment gateway call\n  }\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 7.5 I — Interface Segregation Principle (ISP)\n\n> **\"Clients should not be forced to depend on interfaces they do not use.\"**\n\nDefine small, focused interfaces rather than large, monolithic ones:\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD — Separate interfaces for different concerns\n\n// Read-only operations\ninterface ProjectReader {\n  getById(id: string): Promise<Project>;\n  getAll(filters: ProjectFilters): Promise<PaginatedResult<Project>>;\n}\n\n// Write operations\ninterface ProjectWriter {\n  create(data: CreateProjectDTO): Promise<Project>;\n  update(id: string, data: UpdateProjectDTO): Promise<Project>;\n  delete(id: string): Promise<void>;\n}\n\n// Status management\ninterface ProjectStatusManager {\n  publish(id: string): Promise<Project>;\n  cancel(id: string): Promise<Project>;\n  complete(id: string): Promise<Project>;\n}\n\n// ❌ BAD — One massive interface\ninterface ProjectManager {\n  getById(id: string): Promise<Project>;\n  getAll(filters: ProjectFilters): Promise<PaginatedResult<Project>>;\n  create(data: CreateProjectDTO): Promise<Project>;\n  update(id: string, data: UpdateProjectDTO): Promise<Project>;\n  delete(id: string): Promise<void>;\n  publish(id: string): Promise<Project>;\n  cancel(id: string): Promise<Project>;\n  complete(id: string): Promise<Project>;\n  generateCertificate(id: string): Promise<Certificate>;\n  sendNotification(id: string): Promise<void>;\n  // ... 20 more methods\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 7.6 D — Dependency Inversion Principle (DIP)\n\n> **\"High-level modules should not depend on low-level modules. Both should depend on abstractions.\"**\n\nServices depend on **interfaces (abstractions)**, not on concrete implementations:\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD — Service depends on interface, not implementation\n\n// Define abstraction\ninterface NotificationSender {\n  send(to: string, subject: string, body: string): Promise<void>;\n}\n\n// Concrete implementation (email)\nclass EmailNotificationSender implements NotificationSender {\n  async send(to: string, subject: string, body: string) {\n    await nodemailer.sendMail({ to, subject, html: body });\n  }\n}\n\n// Concrete implementation (in-app — V1.1)\nclass InAppNotificationSender implements NotificationSender {\n  async send(to: string, subject: string, body: string) {\n    await prisma.notification.create({ data: { userId: to, title: subject, body } });\n  }\n}\n\n// Service depends on abstraction\nclass ProjectService {\n  constructor(private notificationSender: NotificationSender) {}\n\n  async acceptProject(projectId: string) {\n    // Business logic...\n    await this.notificationSender.send(\n      student.email,\n      'Project Accepted!',\n      'Your project has been accepted...'\n    );\n  }\n}\n\n// Inject the appropriate implementation\nconst projectService = new ProjectService(new EmailNotificationSender());\n\`\`\`\n\n### 7.7 SOLID Summary for SkillBridge\n\n| Principle | Key Application in SkillBridge                                    |\n| :-------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| **SRP**   | Controller / Service / Validation separation in every module       |\n| **OCP**   | Matching strategy pattern (MVP → V1.1 → V2.0 without modification)|\n| **LSP**   | Escrow providers (Simulated → VNPay) are interchangeable          |\n| **ISP**   | Small, focused interfaces (Reader, Writer, StatusManager)          |\n| **DIP**   | Services depend on interfaces (NotificationSender, EscrowProvider) |\n\n---\n\n## 8. Database & ORM Conventions (Prisma)\n\n### 8.1 Schema Conventions\n\n\`\`\`prisma\n// prisma/schema.prisma\n\n// Table names: snake_case, plural\nmodel User {\n  // Column names: camelCase in Prisma (maps to snake_case in DB)\n  id            String   @id @default(uuid())\n  email         String   @unique\n  passwordHash  String   @map(\"password_hash\")\n  role          UserRole\n  isActive      Boolean  @default(true) @map(\"is_active\")\n  createdAt     DateTime @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt     DateTime @updatedAt @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  // Relations\n  studentProfile StudentProfile?\n  smeProfile     SmeProfile?\n\n  @@map(\"users\")  // Map to snake_case table name\n}\n\n// Enums: PascalCase name, UPPER_SNAKE_CASE values\nenum UserRole {\n  STUDENT\n  SME\n  ADMIN\n}\n\nenum ProjectStatus {\n  DRAFT\n  PENDING_REVIEW\n  PUBLISHED\n  MATCHED\n  IN_PROGRESS\n  COMPLETED\n  CANCELLED\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 8.2 Query Best Practices\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD — Select only needed fields\nconst project = await prisma.project.findUnique({\n  where: { id },\n  select: {\n    id: true,\n    title: true,\n    description: true,\n    budget: true,\n    status: true,\n    sme: {\n      select: { companyName: true },\n    },\n  },\n});\n\n// ✅ GOOD — Avoid N+1 with include\nconst projects = await prisma.project.findMany({\n  include: {\n    milestones: { orderBy: { orderIndex: 'asc' } },\n    sme: { select: { companyName: true } },\n  },\n});\n\n// ❌ BAD — Fetching all columns when not needed\nconst project = await prisma.project.findUnique({ where: { id } });\n\n// ❌ BAD — N+1 queries\nconst projects = await prisma.project.findMany();\nfor (const p of projects) {\n  p.milestones = await prisma.milestone.findMany({ where: { projectId: p.id } });\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 8.3 Migration Rules\n\n| Rule                                               | Rationale                           |\n| :------------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------- |\n| Never modify existing migrations                   | Preserve migration history          |\n| Always create a new migration for schema changes   | Traceability                        |\n| Use descriptive migration names                    | `add_escrow_status_to_projects`     |\n| Include seed data for development                  | Consistent dev environment          |\n| Test migrations on a clean database                | Ensure they run from scratch        |\n\n---\n\n## 9. API Design Conventions\n\n### 9.1 URL Patterns\n\n\`\`\`\n# Resource naming: plural nouns, kebab-case\nGET    /api/v1/projects                    # List resources\nGET    /api/v1/projects/:id                # Get single resource\nPOST   /api/v1/projects                    # Create resource\nPUT    /api/v1/projects/:id                # Update resource\nDELETE /api/v1/projects/:id                # Delete resource\n\n# Nested resources\nGET    /api/v1/projects/:id/milestones     # List milestones for a project\nPOST   /api/v1/projects/:id/applications   # Apply to a project\n\n# Actions (non-CRUD operations)\nPUT    /api/v1/milestones/:id/submit       # Submit milestone for review\nPUT    /api/v1/milestones/:id/review       # Review milestone\nPOST   /api/v1/projects/:id/accept         # Accept project\nPOST   /api/v1/projects/:id/match          # Confirm match\n\`\`\`\n\n### 9.2 Response Format\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// Standardized response helpers — utils/response.ts\n\nexport function sendSuccess<T>(res: Response, data: T, meta?: PaginationMeta) {\n  res.status(200).json({\n    success: true,\n    data,\n    ...(meta && { meta }),\n  });\n}\n\nexport function sendCreated<T>(res: Response, data: T) {\n  res.status(201).json({\n    success: true,\n    data,\n  });\n}\n\nexport function sendNoContent(res: Response) {\n  res.status(204).send();\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 9.3 Error Response Format\n\n\`\`\`json\n{\n  \"success\": false,\n  \"error\": {\n    \"code\": \"VALIDATION_ERROR\",\n    \"message\": \"Invalid input data\",\n    \"details\": [\n      {\n        \"field\": \"title\",\n        \"message\": \"Title must be at least 5 characters\"\n      },\n      {\n        \"field\": \"budget\",\n        \"message\": \"Budget must be a positive number\"\n      }\n    ]\n  }\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n---\n\n## 10. Error Handling\n\n### 10.1 Error Handling Rules\n\n| Rule                                                   | Rationale                              |\n| :----------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------- |\n| Never swallow errors silently                          | All errors must be logged and handled  |\n| Use custom error classes for business errors           | Consistent error types and codes       |\n| Always pass errors to `next()` in Express handlers     | Centralized error handling             |\n| Never expose internal error details to clients         | Security                               |\n| Log full error stack traces server-side                | Debugging                              |\n| Return user-friendly error messages client-side        | UX                                     |\n\n### 10.2 Error Class Hierarchy\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// utils/errors.ts\nexport class AppError extends Error {\n  constructor(\n    public readonly statusCode: number,\n    public readonly code: string,\n    message: string,\n    public readonly details?: unknown\n  ) {\n    super(message);\n    this.name = this.constructor.name;\n    Error.captureStackTrace(this, this.constructor);\n  }\n}\n\nexport class BadRequestError extends AppError {\n  constructor(message: string, details?: unknown) {\n    super(400, 'BAD_REQUEST', message, details);\n  }\n}\n\nexport class UnauthorizedError extends AppError {\n  constructor(message = 'Authentication required') {\n    super(401, 'UNAUTHORIZED', message);\n  }\n}\n\nexport class ForbiddenError extends AppError {\n  constructor(message = 'Access denied') {\n    super(403, 'FORBIDDEN', message);\n  }\n}\n\nexport class NotFoundError extends AppError {\n  constructor(resource: string) {\n    super(404, 'NOT_FOUND', `${resource} not found`);\n  }\n}\n\nexport class ConflictError extends AppError {\n  constructor(message: string) {\n    super(409, 'CONFLICT', message);\n  }\n}\n\nexport class ValidationError extends AppError {\n  constructor(details: unknown) {\n    super(422, 'VALIDATION_ERROR', 'Validation failed', details);\n  }\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 10.3 Global Error Handler\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// middleware/error.middleware.ts\nimport { Request, Response, NextFunction } from 'express';\nimport { AppError } from '@/utils/errors';\nimport { logger } from '@/utils/logger';\n\nexport function errorHandler(\n  err: Error,\n  req: Request,\n  res: Response,\n  _next: NextFunction\n) {\n  // Log the error\n  logger.error('Unhandled error', {\n    error: err.message,\n    stack: err.stack,\n    path: req.path,\n    method: req.method,\n  });\n\n  // Known application error\n  if (err instanceof AppError) {\n    return res.status(err.statusCode).json({\n      success: false,\n      error: {\n        code: err.code,\n        message: err.message,\n        ...(err.details && { details: err.details }),\n      },\n    });\n  }\n\n  // Unknown error — don't expose internals\n  return res.status(500).json({\n    success: false,\n    error: {\n      code: 'INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR',\n      message: 'An unexpected error occurred',\n    },\n  });\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n---\n\n## 11. Testing Standards\n\n### 11.1 Testing Strategy\n\n| Test Type           | Scope                                         | Tool          | Coverage Target |\n| :------------------ | :-------------------------------------------- | :------------ | :-------------- |\n| **Unit Tests**      | Services, utilities, validation               | Jest          | ≥ 60% (core)    |\n| **Integration Tests** | API endpoints (route → DB)                 | Jest + Supertest | Key flows    |\n| **E2E Tests**       | Critical user flows                           | Playwright    | Deferred to V1.1 |\n\n### 11.2 Test File Naming\n\n| Convention                        | Example                              |\n| :-------------------------------- | :----------------------------------- |\n| Unit test: `*.test.ts`            | `project.service.test.ts`            |\n| Integration test: `*.integration.test.ts` | `project.routes.integration.test.ts` |\n| Test utility: `*.helper.ts`       | `auth.helper.ts`                     |\n\n### 11.3 Test Structure (AAA Pattern)\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// project.service.test.ts\nimport { ProjectService } from './project.service';\n\ndescribe('ProjectService', () => {\n  let service: ProjectService;\n\n  beforeEach(() => {\n    service = new ProjectService();\n  });\n\n  describe('createProject', () => {\n    it('should create a project with valid data', async () => {\n      // Arrange\n      const smeId = 'sme-uuid-123';\n      const dto = {\n        title: 'Build Landing Page',\n        description: 'Create a responsive landing page for our F&B business',\n        requiredSkills: ['html', 'css', 'javascript'],\n        budget: 3000000,\n        durationWeeks: 2,\n        milestones: [\n          { title: 'Design mockup', description: 'Create Figma design', deadline: '2026-09-01' },\n          { title: 'Development', description: 'Code the page', deadline: '2026-09-15' },\n        ],\n      };\n\n      // Act\n      const project = await service.createProject(smeId, dto);\n\n      // Assert\n      expect(project).toBeDefined();\n      expect(project.title).toBe(dto.title);\n      expect(project.status).toBe('PENDING_REVIEW');\n      expect(project.milestones).toHaveLength(2);\n    });\n\n    it('should throw ValidationError when title is too short', async () => {\n      // Arrange\n      const dto = { title: 'Hi', /* ... */ };\n\n      // Act & Assert\n      await expect(service.createProject('sme-id', dto as any))\n        .rejects.toThrow(ValidationError);\n    });\n  });\n});\n\`\`\`\n\n### 11.4 Testing Rules\n\n| Rule                                              | Rationale                              |\n| :------------------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------- |\n| Each test has a single assertion focus             | Clear failure messages                 |\n| Use descriptive test names (`should...when...`)    | Self-documenting tests                 |\n| Mock external dependencies (DB, email)             | Fast, isolated tests                   |\n| Never test implementation details                  | Test behavior, not internals           |\n| Clean up test data in `afterEach`/`afterAll`       | Independent test execution             |\n\n---\n\n## 12. Git Workflow & Version Control\n\n### 12.1 Branch Strategy\n\n\`\`\`mermaid\ngitgraph\n    commit id: \"Initial setup\"\n    branch develop\n    checkout develop\n    commit id: \"feat: auth module\"\n    commit id: \"feat: project module\"\n    branch feature/milestone-tracking\n    checkout feature/milestone-tracking\n    commit id: \"feat: milestone CRUD\"\n    commit id: \"feat: milestone status\"\n    checkout develop\n    merge feature/milestone-tracking id: \"Merge: milestone tracking\"\n    checkout main\n    merge develop id: \"Release v0.1.0 (MVP)\"\n\`\`\`\n\n| Branch         | Purpose                                | Source      | Merges Into |\n| :------------- | :------------------------------------- | :---------- | :---------- |\n| `main`         | Production-ready code                  | —           | —           |\n| `develop`      | Integration branch for features        | `main`      | `main`      |\n| `feature/*`    | New feature development                | `develop`   | `develop`   |\n| `bugfix/*`     | Bug fixes                              | `develop`   | `develop`   |\n| `hotfix/*`     | Critical production fixes              | `main`      | `main` + `develop` |\n\n### 12.2 Commit Message Convention\n\nFollow [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/):\n\n\`\`\`\n<type>(<scope>): <description>\n\n[optional body]\n\n[optional footer]\n\`\`\`\n\n| Type       | When to Use                                  | Example                                      |\n| :--------- | :------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------- |\n| `feat`     | New feature                                  | `feat(project): add milestone tracking`      |\n| `fix`      | Bug fix                                      | `fix(auth): resolve JWT expiration issue`    |\n| `docs`     | Documentation changes                        | `docs: update SRS with escrow requirements`  |\n| `style`    | Formatting, missing semicolons (no logic change) | `style: format project service`          |\n| `refactor` | Code change that neither fixes nor adds      | `refactor(matching): extract strategy pattern` |\n| `test`     | Adding or updating tests                     | `test(project): add createProject unit tests`|\n| `chore`    | Maintenance tasks, dependencies              | `chore: update Prisma to v5.10`              |\n\n### 12.3 Pull Request Rules\n\n| Rule                                              | Requirement                            |\n| :------------------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------- |\n| PR title follows commit convention                | `feat(module): description`            |\n| PR description explains **what** and **why**      | Required                               |\n| All CI checks pass                                | Required                               |\n| At least 1 code review approval                   | Required                               |\n| No direct commits to `main` or `develop`          | Required                               |\n| PR size: ≤ 400 lines changed                      | Recommended                            |\n| Linked to issue/task                              | Recommended                            |\n\n### 12.4 .gitignore Essentials\n\n\`\`\`gitignore\n# Dependencies\nnode_modules/\n\n# Environment\n.env\n.env.local\n.env.*.local\n\n# Build output\n.next/\ndist/\nbuild/\n\n# IDE\n.vscode/\n.idea/\n*.swp\n\n# OS\n.DS_Store\nThumbs.db\n\n# Logs\n*.log\nnpm-debug.log*\n\n# Prisma\nprisma/migrations/**/migration_lock.toml\n\n# Test coverage\ncoverage/\n\`\`\`\n\n---\n\n## 13. Code Review Checklist\n\n### 13.1 Reviewer Checklist\n\nWhen reviewing a Pull Request, check the following:\n\n**Correctness**\n- [ ] Does the code do what the PR description says?\n- [ ] Are edge cases handled?\n- [ ] Are error conditions handled properly?\n\n**Design**\n- [ ] Does it follow the layered architecture (Controller → Service → DAL)?\n- [ ] Does it respect SOLID principles?\n- [ ] Is there unnecessary complexity?\n- [ ] Are there any hard-coded values that should be constants/config?\n\n**Code Quality**\n- [ ] Does it follow naming conventions?\n- [ ] Is the code readable without excessive comments?\n- [ ] Are functions/components small and focused?\n- [ ] Are TypeScript types used properly (no `any`)?\n\n**Security**\n- [ ] Are inputs validated?\n- [ ] Is authorization checked (correct role)?\n- [ ] Are sensitive data (passwords, tokens) handled properly?\n- [ ] Is there any SQL injection or XSS risk?\n\n**Testing**\n- [ ] Are there tests for new business logic?\n- [ ] Do existing tests still pass?\n- [ ] Are test names descriptive?\n\n**Documentation**\n- [ ] Are complex logic sections documented?\n- [ ] Is the API documentation updated?\n- [ ] Are type definitions complete?\n\n---\n\n## 14. Documentation Standards\n\n### 14.1 Code Comments\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n// ✅ GOOD — Explain WHY, not WHAT\n// Use simulated escrow in MVP because real payment gateway\n// integration requires Legal Advisor approval (planned for V1.1)\nasync function depositEscrow(projectId: string, amount: number) {\n  return updateEscrowStatus(projectId, 'DEPOSITED');\n}\n\n// ❌ BAD — States the obvious (what the code does)\n// This function updates the escrow status\nasync function depositEscrow(projectId: string, amount: number) {\n  return updateEscrowStatus(projectId, 'DEPOSITED');\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 14.2 JSDoc for Public APIs\n\n\`\`\`typescript\n/**\n * Creates a new project posting for an SME.\n *\n * The project starts in PENDING_REVIEW status and must be approved\n * by an admin before it becomes visible to students.\n *\n * @param smeId - The UUID of the SME creating the project\n * @param dto - The project creation data\n * @returns The created project with milestones\n * @throws {NotFoundError} If the SME profile is not found\n * @throws {ValidationError} If the input data is invalid\n *\n * @example\n * \`\`\`typescript\n * const project = await projectService.createProject('sme-uuid', {\n *   title: 'Build Landing Page',\n *   description: 'Create a responsive landing page...',\n *   requiredSkills: ['html', 'css', 'javascript'],\n *   budget: 3000000,\n *   durationWeeks: 2,\n *   milestones: [{ title: 'Design', description: '...', deadline: '2026-09-01' }],\n * });\n * \`\`\`\n */\nasync createProject(smeId: string, dto: CreateProjectDTO): Promise<Project> {\n  // ...\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 14.3 README Requirements\n\nEvery major directory SHOULD have a brief `README.md`:\n\n\`\`\`markdown\n# Module: Project\n\nHandles all project-related operations including CRUD, status management,\nand project lifecycle transitions.\n\n## Files\n\n| File                    | Purpose                                  |\n| ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |\n| project.controller.ts   | HTTP request/response handling            |\n| project.service.ts      | Business logic                            |\n| project.routes.ts       | Route definitions                         |\n| project.validation.ts   | Zod schemas for input validation          |\n| project.types.ts        | TypeScript type definitions               |\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n- Projects go through a lifecycle: DRAFT → PENDING_REVIEW → PUBLISHED → MATCHED → IN_PROGRESS → COMPLETED\n- Escrow status is simulated in MVP (status flags only, no real payments)\n\`\`\`\n\n---\n\n## 15. Tooling & Configuration\n\n### 15.1 ESLint Configuration\n\n\`\`\`javascript\n// .eslintrc.js\nmodule.exports = {\n  root: true,\n  parser: '@typescript-eslint/parser',\n  plugins: ['@typescript-eslint'],\n  extends: [\n    'eslint:recommended',\n    'plugin:@typescript-eslint/recommended',\n    'plugin:@typescript-eslint/recommended-requiring-type-checking',\n    'prettier', // Must be last to disable conflicting rules\n  ],\n  rules: {\n    // TypeScript\n    '@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any': 'error',\n    '@typescript-eslint/explicit-function-return-type': 'off',\n    '@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars': ['error', { argsIgnorePattern: '^_' }],\n\n    // General\n    'no-console': ['warn', { allow: ['warn', 'error'] }],\n    'prefer-const': 'error',\n    'no-var': 'error',\n    'eqeqeq': ['error', 'always'],\n    'curly': ['error', 'all'],\n  },\n};\n\`\`\`\n\n### 15.2 Prettier Configuration\n\n\`\`\`json\n// .prettierrc\n{\n  \"semi\": true,\n  \"trailingComma\": \"all\",\n  \"singleQuote\": true,\n  \"printWidth\": 100,\n  \"tabWidth\": 2,\n  \"useTabs\": false,\n  \"bracketSpacing\": true,\n  \"arrowParens\": \"always\",\n  \"endOfLine\": \"lf\"\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n### 15.3 Husky + lint-staged\n\n\`\`\`json\n// package.json\n{\n  \"lint-staged\": {\n    \"*.{ts,tsx}\": [\n      \"eslint --fix\",\n      \"prettier --write\"\n    ],\n    \"*.{json,md,css}\": [\n      \"prettier --write\"\n    ]\n  }\n}\n\`\`\`\n\n\`\`\`bash\n# .husky/pre-commit\n#!/usr/bin/env sh\n. \"$(dirname -- \"$0\")/_/husky.sh\"\nnpx lint-staged\n\`\`\`\n\n### 15.4 VS Code Settings (Recommended)\n\n\`\`\`jsonc\n// .vscode/settings.json\n{\n  \"editor.formatOnSave\": true,\n  \"editor.defaultFormatter\": \"esbenp.prettier-vscode\",\n <USER_REQUEST>
You are acting as a Senior Software Architect and Technical Documentation Reviewer.

I have three technical documents for the SkillBridge project:

1. Software Requirements Specification (SRS)
2. Software Architecture Document (SAD)
3. Coding Standards

The current documents are too long and contain duplicated or unnecessary information. I want you to refactor them into a concise, professional, maintainable documentation set suitable for a university capstone project and an MVP-stage startup.

IMPORTANT:
Do NOT rewrite the project from scratch.
Instead, review the existing documents section by section and decide whether each section should be:

- Keep as-is
- Shorten
- Merge with another section
- Remove completely

For every removed or merged section, briefly explain why.

---

# General Principles

The documentation should satisfy these goals:

- Clear responsibilities between documents
- No duplicated information
- Easy for new developers to understand
- Focus on architecture and engineering decisions
- Avoid explaining common knowledge
- Suitable for an MVP, not enterprise-level documentation

Do not remove information that affects implementation.

Remove information that only explains obvious concepts or repeats existing content.

---

# Responsibilities of each document

## SRS

The SRS should only describe WHAT the system must do.

Keep:

- Functional Requirements
- Non-functional Requirements
- User Roles
- Business Rules
- Use Cases (**must keep**)
- API Design (**must keep**)
- Constraints
- Assumptions
- Glossary (if useful)

Remove or shorten:

- Long explanations about technologies
- Architecture discussions
- UI implementation details
- Database implementation explanations
- Coding conventions
- Anything belonging to SAD

---

## Software Architecture Document (SAD)

The SAD should describe HOW the system is built.

Keep:

- Architecture Overview
- Architecture Pattern
- System Context
- Component Diagram
- Deployment Diagram
- Database Design
- Technology Stack
- Security Architecture
- API Architecture
- Folder Structure
- Design Decisions
- External Services

Shorten:

- Large theoretical explanations
- Definitions everyone already knows
- Repeated descriptions of React, Node.js, PostgreSQL, etc.

Remove:

- Requirement descriptions already existing in SRS
- Business process descriptions
- User stories
- Acceptance criteria
- Requirement traceability

---

## Coding Standards

Keep only conventions that cannot be enforced automatically.

Keep:

- Project folder structure
- Naming conventions
- Component organization
- Service organization
- API organization
- Git workflow
- Branch naming
- Commit convention
- Pull Request convention
- Code Review checklist
- Documentation convention

Remove:

- Formatting rules
- Semicolon rules
- Quote rules
- Indentation
- Trailing commas
- Import ordering
- Unused variables
- const vs let
- Console.log rules
- Any rule that ESLint or Prettier can enforce automatically

Instead, simply mention that formatting and basic code style are enforced through ESLint + Prettier.

---

# Additional Instructions

Do NOT remove important implementation decisions.

Prefer concise bullet points over long paragraphs.

Avoid duplicated explanations across documents.

If two sections overlap, keep the more appropriate one and remove the duplicate.

---

# Required Output

For each document:

1. Overall evaluation
2. Sections to KEEP
3. Sections to SHORTEN
4. Sections to REMOVE
5. Sections to MERGE
6. Explanation for every change
7. Estimated page reduction
8. Produce the final revised document after all changes

Maintain all existing headings unless they are removed or merged.
